# DEAR LADIES AND GENTLEMEN,

in 2013, we were consistent in further building the value of the ORLEN Group. We operated in line with our strategy, whose objectives include not only maintaining an optimum liquidity position and cost control, diversifying sources of financing and strengthening our core businesses, but also intensive growth in the power and upstream segments. Importantly, our capital expenditure depends on mac­ roeconomic conditions.

### LETTER FROM 
THE CHAIRWOMAN 
OF THE PKN ORLEN 
SUPERVISORY BOARD

## PKN ORLEN Annual Report 2013


---
*Page 8*


Our strategy has proved effective. Last year, despite the unstable market situation and macroeconomic pressures, the Group’s total sales volumes increased by 0.6 m tonnes, to nearly 36 m tonnes. The Group posted revenue in excess of PLN 113 bn and generated LIFO-based EBITDA of PLN 3.2 bn.

Our investments in the upstream segment have positioned us as a company of global reach. This was made possible by the acquisition of Tri Oil Resources, a Canadian production company with oil and gas producing assets in the most technologically advanced region of the world. The acquisition provided us with provided us with access to unique expertise, which we will further develop and adapt to the local unconventional hydrocarbon production conditions. PKN ORLEN has also been consistently pursuing shale gas exploration projects in Poland, where we have drilled 10 exploratory wells to date.

Our major power generation project, started in 2013, is the CCGT unit in Włocławek. When complete, the new unit will co-generate electricity and heat for the ANWIL Group, PKN ORLEN and third-party customers. The project is scheduled to be commissioned in late 2015.

Given the persistently unstable conditions in foreign markets, we focused on strengthening the position of our Group companies, which was the reason behind the purchase of 16.4% of shares in Česká Rafinérská by the Unipetrol Group. Increasing our equity interest in the company has significantly improved our ability to manage the business in an effective manner.

Another important development in 2013 was the successful issuance of retail bonds, which were introduced to trading on the Catalyst market. Strong investor interest in our retail bond programme, finalised in 2014, is an endorsement of PKN ORLEN as a solid and reliable partner and a trusted market participant.

In its pursuit of the strategic objectives, the Company relies on the corporate value system approved in 2012 and implemented across the Group last year. In an effort to create a coherent and transparent vision of our operations, we are guided by the following values: responsibility, growth, people, energy and reliability.

Last year proved that our current strategy is the optimum one, as it gives the Company operational stability even amid adverse macroeconomic conditions. I am certain that the direction we have chosen to follow will contribute to creating long-term value for the ORLEN Group across all its segments.

Angelina Sarota Chairwoman of the Supervisory Board

Dariusz Jacek Krawiec was appointed President of the Management Board and Chief Executive Officer on September 18 th 2008. On March 24 th 2011, the Supervisory Board reappointed him as President of the Management Board for another term of office.

On March 6 th 2014, Mr Krawiec was once again reappointed by the Supervisory Board for a further, third, term of office to begin on the date following the Company’s Annual General Meeting which approves the 2013 financial statements.

He is a graduate of the Poznań University of Economics. From 1992 to 1997, he worked for Bank PEKAO S.A. and Ernst & Young and Price Waterhouse consulting firms. In 1998, he worked with the UK branch of Japanese investment bank Nomura plc, headquartered in London, where he was responsible for the Polish market. From 1998 to 2002, he served as President of the Management Board and CEO of Impexmetal S.A. In 2002, he was appointed President of the Management Board of Elektrim S.A. From 2003 to 2004, he was Managing Director at Sindicatum Ltd London, and between 2006 and 2008 he served as President of the Management Board of Action S.A. Mr Krawiec has a wealth of experience working on corporate supervisory bodies. He has chaired the supervisory boards of Huta Aluminium Konin S.A., Metalexfrance S.A. of Paris, S and I SA of Lausanne, and ce-market.com S.A., and also has served as Member of the Supervisory Boards of Impexmetal S.A., Elektrim S.A., PTC Sp. z o.o., Elektrim Telekomunikacja Sp. z o.o., Elektrim Magadex S.A., Elektrim Volt S.A. and PTE AIG. Currently, he serves as Chairman of the Supervisory Board of Unipetrol, a.s.

Sławomir Jędrzejczyk was appointed Member of the PKN ORLEN Management Board in June 2008. Until September 2008, he served as Member of the PKN ORLEN Management Board, and on September 18 th he became its Vice-President. On March 24 th 2011, the Supervisory Board reappointed Mr Jędrzejczyk as Vice-President of the PKN ORLEN Management Board for the next three-year term of office, to commence June 30 th 2011.

On March 6 th 2014, Mr Jędrzejczyk was reappointed by the Supervisory Board as Vice-President of the Management Board for another, third, term of office, to begin on the date following the Company’s Annual General Meeting which approves the 2013 financial statements.

Sławomir Jędrzejczyk is responsible for finance, financial controlling, accounting, supply chain management, investor relations, M&A and IT. His key responsibilities include implementing strategies geared towards increasing company value, building capital market relations, securing financing, and increasing cash flows through operating excellence, divestments, and projects involving working capital. Currently, he serves as Deputy Chairman of the Supervisory Board of Unipetrol, a.s. Since January 2014, he has been Member of the Board of Directors of Tri Oil Resources Ltd., Canada. Mr Jędrzejczyk graduated from the Łódź University of Technology and obtained the title of British Certified Auditor from the Association of Chartered Certified Accountants. From 2005 to 2008, he served as President of the Management Board and CEO of Emitel. Earlier, he worked for companies listed on the Warsaw Stock Exchange, as Head of the Financial Controlling Division of Telekomunikacja Polska S.A., as Member of the Management Board and Chief Financial Officer at Impexmetal S.A., and in the Audit and Business Consulting Division of Price Waterhouse.

# PKN ORLEN MANAGEMENT BOARD

Piotr Chełmiński was appointed Member of the Management Board of PKN ORLEN on March 10 th 2012.

On March 6 th 2014, Mr Chełmiński was reappointed by the Supervisory Board as Member of the Management Board for another three-year term of office, to begin on the date following the Company’s Annual General Meeting which approves the 2013 financial statements.

He is a graduate of the Warsaw University of Life Sciences, and has experience working on management boards of Polish and foreign companies, including companies listed on the stock exchange. From 1995 to 1996, Mr Chełmiński worked as Vice-President for Sales, Marketing and Export in Okocimskie Zakłady Piwowarskie S.A., and from 1996 to 1999 he held the position of Regional Director for Central and Eastern Europe in Eckes Granini Gmb H & Co.KG, and President of its subsidiary, Aronia S.A. From 1999 to 2001, he was Member of the Management Board of Browar Dojlidy Sp. z o.o., and from 2001 to 2002 he held the positions of Member of the Management Board and Member of the Super­ visory Board of Werner & Merz Polska Sp. z o.o. In 2001−2006, his served as Member of the Management Board and Member of the Supervisory Board of Kamis-Przyprawy S.A., where he was responsible for direct operational supervision of Sales and Marketing. From 2006 to 2009, Mr Chełmiński held the position of Vice-President for Sales and Marketing at Gamet S.A. in Toruń, and Member of the Management Board of Gamet Holdings S.A. of Luxembourg. In December 2009, he took up the position of President of the Management Board and CEO at Unipetrol a.s., which he held until April 2013. Currently, he serves as Chairman of the Supervisory Board of ANWIL S.A., Deputy Chairman of the Supervisory Board of Basell Orlen Polyolefins Sp. z o.o., and Member of the Supervisory Board of Uni­ petrol, a.s.

Mr Krystian Pater was appointed as Member of the Management Board in March 2007. On March 24 th 2011, the PKN ORLEN Supervisory Board adopted a resolution to reappoint him as Member of the PKN ORLEN Management Board for the next term of office, beginning on June 30 th 2011.

On March 6 th 2014, Mr Pater was reappointed by the Supervisory Board as Member of the Management Board for another three-year term of office, to begin on the date following the Company’s Annual General Meeting which approves the 2013 financial statements.

He is a graduate of the Nicolaus Copernicus University in Toruń, Faculty of Chemistry. He completed postgraduate courses in Chemical Engineering and Equipment at the Warsaw University of Technology (1989), Management and Marketing at the Paweł Włodkowic University College in Płock (1997), Petroleum Industry Management (1998), and Company Value Management (2001–2002) at the Warsaw School of Economics. Since 1993 he has worked at Petrochemia Płock S.A., and then PKN ORLEN, where from 2005 to 2007 he was Executive Director for Refinery Production. Currently, he serves as Member of the Management Board of AB ORLEN Lietuva and Member of the Supervisory Board of Unipetrol, a.s., as well as Member of the Management Board of CONCAWE, and Chairman of the Association of Oil Industry Workers in Płock.

Mr Marek Podstawa was appointed Member of the Management Board for Sales by the Supervisory Board on March 19 th 2012.

On March 6 th 2014, Mr Podstawa was reappointed by the Supervisory Board as Member of the Management Board for another three-year term of office, to begin on the date following the Company’s Annual General Meeting which approves the 2013 financial statements.

Since January 2009, Mr Podstawa has held the position of Executive Director, Retail Sales. He is a graduate of the AGH University of Science and Technology in Kraków, and holds an MBA from the University of Minnesota and the Warsaw School of Economics. He has a wealth of experience in international team management, strategy development, finance, project management, and crisis management. From 1990 to 1992, he worked at the Central Plant of Metallurgy Automation, and then at Du Pont Conoco Poland until 1996. After the company’s transformation into Conoco Phillips, until 2008 he held positions in retail and wholesale trade, marketing and business development, standardisation of finance and management systems, in Europe and in the U.S. He then moved up from the position of Coordinator of Marketing and Refining Projects to Director for Wholesale Programmes, and then to the role of Director for Strategic Planning at the Conoco Phillips headquarters in Houston, Texas. From 2009 to 2010, Mr Podstawa was Member of the Management Board of Benzina, s.r.o. Currently, he serves as Chairman of the Supervisory Board of ORLEN Deutschland Gmb H.

# DEAR LADIES AND GENTLEMEN,

2013 was the first year of the Company’s new strategy until 2017, in which we concentrate on the development of two new segments – oil and gas exploration and production, and power generation – while maintaining solid financial security and the operational excellence in the existing areas of our business. Last year, delivering on one of our key strategic objectives, we paid out a dividend – for the first time since 2008 – at a yield of 3.8%, based on the average share price in 2012.

### LETTER FROM THE PRESIDENT OF THE MANAGEMENT BOARD AND CEO OF PKN ORLEN

## LETTER FROM 
THE PRESIDENT 
OF THE MANAGEMENT

# BOARD AND CEO

# OF PKN ORLEN


---
*Page 13*


In 2013, the total sales volume of the ORLEN Group was nearly 36 m tonnes, which translated into a revenue of PLN 113 bn and LIFO-based EBITDA of PLN 3.2 bn. We achieved a record-high retail profit of PLN 1.3 bn, and an excellent result in the petrochemical segment which came in at more than PLN 2 bn, while refining profits were down by PLN 0.5 bn on the back of unfavourable macroeconomic conditions.

With ongoing confidence in the potential of the retail segment, we continued to expand our service station network both in Poland and abroad, reaching 2,706 stations as the end of 2013. We also vigorously developed our non-fuel services, opening 234 new Stop Cafe and Stop Cafe Bistro catering outlets in 2013, which increased the network size to a total of 1,047 locations.

Leveraging our potential as the largest petrochemical company in the region, in Poland we reported higher sales of polymers, monomers, aromatic hydrocarbons, plastics and PTA. However, in the Czech market our sales volumes declined.

The refining segment’s weaker performance was attributable to adverse macroeconomic conditions, with refining margins reaching their 10-year low in Q 4 2013 and diesel oil sales negatively affected by the fuels grey market. However, despite those challenges, the Company’s total sales volumes grew in Poland and on ORLEN Lietuva’s markets, while the Czech market fell.

Last year, in line with our current strategy objectives, which include revenue source diversification, we actively developed our upstream segment. To date, we have drilled ten unconventional gas exploratory wells in Poland, including three horizontal ones, and performed two hydraulic fracturing treatments of horizontal well sections. The acquisition of Tri Oil Resources, a Canadian production company, marked a breakthrough in the development of the ORLEN Group’s upstream segment making us a truly global company. Tri Oil not only operates producing oil and gas wells, but also brings unique expertise which is key for our shale gas exploration projects in Poland. This knowledge capital is particularly valuable to PKN ORLEN as Poland’s leading unconventional hydrocarbon exploration company.

The ORLEN Group’s major power generating project in 2013 was the CCGT unit in Włocławek, the construction of which began in spring last year. In 2015, the new unit will commence co-generation of electricity and heat for the ANWIL Group, PKN ORLEN and also third-party customers.

Our cash flows from operating activities came in at PLN 5.7 bn, enabling the pursuit of our investment plans, payment of a dividend and further reduction of our debt, which at the end of 2013 was PLN 4.6 bn, having decreased PLN 2.1 bn year on year.

Aiming to diversify our finance sources, and encouraged by the resoundingly successful corporate bonds issue the previous year, we issued PLN 0.7 bn worth of retail bonds, well received by the market. We also sold another portion of our mandatory oil stocks, thus freeing up PLN 1 bn of the Group’s assets. It is my great pleasure to report that the Company’s consistent strategy implementation and flexible investment policy have been recognised by rating agencies. Fitch Ratings and Moody’s Investors Service raised PKN ORLEN’s credit rating to BBB- and Baa 3, respectively. Thus, the Company regained its investment – grade rating from the two world’s leading rating agencies.

As the ORLEN Group recognises feedstock supply security as the key to its stability, last year we signed our first direct supply contracts with a crude oil producer, securing deliveries to the Płock refinery and to the Unipetrol Group.

Bearing in mind the growing importance of natural gas, we are also pursuing a number of strategic projects to diversify our sources of this feedstock. To this end, in 2013 we signed short-term contracts with five natural gas suppliers, followed in early 2014 by new contracts with a further six suppliers. Furthermore, we are expanding our range of modern trading channels, for instance by acquiring a licence to trade in natural gas on the EEX (European Energy Exchange) in Leipzig and becoming a member of the Polish Power Exchange. We are convinced that these measures will position us well to benefit from the opportunities offered by the deregulated gas market in Poland.

Last year, we also focused on the development of direct relations with our trading partners. We optimised our fuel and fuel oil wholesale framework by transferring small and medium-sized accounts to ORLEN Paliwa, which allowed us to better leverage our sales potential, benefit from economies of scale and improve our customer service flexibility.

## 12

## PKN ORLEN Annual Report 2013


---
*Page 14*


In early 2014, PKN ORLEN took over Petrolot’s aviation fuel sales to incorporate them under its wholesale operations, while Petrolot became an independent operator providing aviation fuel storage and aircraft refuelling services to fuel suppliers at largest airports.

As regards supplier relations, we consolidated the purchasing processes at 14 ORLEN Group companies using the innovative CONNECT Procurement Platform.

In November 2013, we initiated the acquisition of 16.4% of shares in Česká Rafinérská from Shell. Following its closing in early 2014, this USD 27 m transaction raised our ownership interest in Česká Rafinérská to 67.6%, significantly improving our ability to effectively manage the company.

Our dynamic growth was recognised by independent market experts. PKN ORLEN topped the Euro­money list of the best-managed oil companies, was ranked first in the Coface ranking of the largest CEE companies for the eighth consecutive time, and was listed high, in 79 th position, in Platts’ Global Energy Company Rankings. For seven consecutive editions, we have remained part of the elite group of companies included in the RESPECT Index of the Warsaw Stock Exchange. Our efforts in the field of information reporting were appreciated as well, as we were named ‘The Best of The Best’ for our financial reports published on the WSE. In a ranking list put together by Rzeczpospolita, ORLEN emerged as the most valuable Polish brand, with its value estimated at PLN 3.9 bn.

It was only natural that being the largest company in Poland we should be active in debates on the most pressing social and economic issues, both in Poland and in the international arena. Last year’s key discussions featuring representatives of the ORLEN Group included those held at the European Financial Congress, the Economic Forum in Krynica and the European Forum of New Ideas. Our delegates also participated in conferences abroad, such as CERAWeek in Houston and the St. Petersburg International Economic Forum, where the most important global economic issues are discussed.

During the past year we expanded the range of our sponsorship projects. In addition to the VERVA Street Racing and Poles with Verve initiatives, we implemented a project aimed at promoting healthy lifestyles – the ORLEN Warsaw Marathon. The event generated great interest, with the first edition attracting more than 26 thousand runners both from Poland and around the world, as well as thousands of spectators. Encouraged by this success, we decided to organise the second edition of the National Running Day in 2014, which also attracted crowds of running enthusiasts.

Without any doubt, 2013 was a landmark year in the Company’s history. Thanks to the commitment of our Employees, the highest governance standards and a well-defined growth strategy, we were not only able to prove that success is possible in a difficult market environment, but also that we are capable of effectively expanding our business and delivering on our strategic objectives, thus creating strong value-building foundations for the coming years.

I would like to offer my sincere thanks to Supervisory Board members, Management Board members, the Management Staff and all Employees, as it is their hard work that warrants an optimistic future outlook. I am also grateful to you, our Shareholders, for the trust you have placed in us when making your investment decisions.

## Jacek Krawiec

## President of the Management Board, CEO
PKN ORLEN

## 13

### LETTER FROM THE PRESIDENT OF THE MANAGEMENT BOARD AND CEO OF PKN ORLEN


---
*Page 15*


Rankings recently published by the British financial magazine Euromoney show PKN ORLEN AS THE BEST managed oil company in the region. PKN ORLEN has also been named the most valuable Polish brand and the best employer. For years now we have TOPPED the list of largest Polish companies in rankings published by the Polityka weekly MAGAZINE and RZECZPOSPOLITA DAILY NEWSPAPER, and were recently awarded the prestigious title of ‘World’s Most Ethical Company’ by an international panel of experts at the US Ethisphere Institute. This was a first for any company in Central and Eastern Europe. PKN ORLEN has been listed on the Warsaw Stock Exchange for 15 years, and is currently included in the WIG 20 and WIG 30 indices, as well as the high-profile RESPECT Index. Our investor relations and quality of our financial reports published on the WSE have been viewed for years by both analysts and investors as being among the best of its kind.

We operate six refineries and the region’s largest network of service stations located in Poland, the Czech Republic, Germany and Lithuania. We process crude oil into gasolines, diesel oil, fuel oil and aviation fuel. We are also a leading producer of petrochemicals, and our products are used as basic feedstocks by a large number of chemical companies.

In line with our strategy for gradual transfor­ mation of the Group into an energy conglomerate, we consistently develop our hydrocarbon explo- ration and production (upstream) and power segments. We are building a 463 MWe CCGT unit in Włocławek, and a similar project in Płock is in the development phase. We hold ten licences for oil and gas exploration throughout the country. One of our priorities is to explore for and extract natural gas from unconventional sources in Poland. We are also involved in hydrocarbon production from unconventional deposits in Canada through our subsidiary, Tri Oil.

PKN ORLEN boasts the regions’ largest network of nearly 2,700 modern service stations located in Poland, Germany, the Czech Republic and Lithuania. Our retail network is well supported by the logistics infrastructure, including surface and underground storage depots and a long-distance pipeline network.

Our products and services have been, for years, highly esteemed by retail and institutional cus- tomers alike. ORLEN has time and again topped the prestigious charts of most valuable Polish brands, published by Rzeczpospolita, and holds the number one position in the European Trusted Brands survey.

We are committed to sustainable development and attach a lot of importance to our impact on the internal and external environment. People are our most important asset, and this is the case no matter who they are − customers, employees or stakeholders. Our success, created every single day through the commitment of nearly 22,000 employees, is built on respect for ethics and the key values of the ORLEN Group.

## ORLEN Group 
VALUES

Strategic companies, such as PKN ORLEN, are responsible not only for the development of eco- nomic standards, but also for the creation of new standards of ethical practice in businesses every- where. Companies like ours must set standards in not only best practice uses of modern financial tools, but also in production and marketing innovation, as well as effective communication. Strategic companies should also dictate direction when it comes to how business is conducted – they must clearly define the values which form the backbone of the company. But more than this, they are required to protect those values and live by them day-to-day.

The ‘PKN ORLEN Code of Conduct’, intro- duced in 2012, was developed in cooperation with and for all of our employees. It provides guidelines on relations inside PKN ORLEN itself as well as externally with trading partners, local communities, and competitors. Eighteen months on from the launch of the project, the ORLEN values of Responsibility, Development, People, Energy and Reliability have become an indelible part of our shared corporate culture. An Ethics Officer and a Human Capital Committee are now active at PKN ORLEN. We have organised a series of training sessions for employees and manage- ment, and launched an electronic training platform, remaining open for any discussions or employee concerns on corporate values or ethical conduct.

In 2013, the process of implementation of ethical practice solutions at individual ORLEN Group companies began, with each of the companies pre- sented with the task of adapting the ‘PKN ORLEN Code of Conduct’ to their specific requirements. The mission statements of the companies, following review by the PKN ORLEN Ethics Committee, became more dynamic. A company motto was introduced – ‘ORLEN Group – We Fuel the Future’ − emphasising the unity and integrity of our operations.

## ORLEN GROUP 
ON THE CAPITAL MARKET

PKN ORLEN shares are listed on the main market of the Warsaw Stock Exchange in a continuous trading system, and are included in the WIG, WIG 20, WIG 30 and WIG-Poland indices, as well as in the WIG-Paliwa index of fuel sec- tor companies. Since November 19 th 2009, PKN ORLEN shares have also been a constituent of the RESPECT Index of socially responsible com- panies. Following the index update in 2013, PKN ORLEN was, for the seventh time, included in this elite group. PKN ORLEN retail bonds have been listed on the Warsaw Stock Exchange’s Catalyst market since June 2013. PKN ORLEN corporate bonds have also been traded here since 2012.

In 2013, the blue-chip WIG 20 index fell (-) 7% (yoy), while the WIG index recorded an 8.1% gain. The average annual PKN ORLEN share price in 2013 grew by 22.1% (yoy), while the PKN ORLEN share price as at the end of 2013 fell by (-) 17.2% (yoy). In 2013, 226 m PKN ORLEN shares were traded on the main market, down (-) 21% on the trading volume recorded in 2012.

PKN ORLEN’s share capital is PLN 534,636,326.25 and comprises 427,709,061 ordinary bearer shares with par value of PLN 1.25 per share. No restrictions exist as to the transferability of PKN ORLEN shares.

In 1999, PKN ORLEN listed its shares as Global Depositary Receipts (GDRs) on the London Stock Exchange, and in 2001 its American Depositary Receipts (ADRs) were introduced to trading on the OTC market in the US. The trading unit on the OTC market was one American Depositary Receipt (one ADR). Given the diminishing inves- tor interest in the depositary receipts, the Com- pany decided to end both programmes in 2012. The GDR depository agreement was terminated as of February 27 th 2013, and the ADR depository agreement was terminated as of March 4 th 2013.

On the capital market, we are also represented by one of our subsidiaries, Unipetrol, a.s., which is listed on the main market of the Prague Stock Exchange and the Czech OTC market (RM-SYSTÉM, a.s.). The company’s share capital comprises 181,334,764 ordinary shares with a par value of CZK 100 per share, of which 67,108,265 shares (or 37.01% of the share capital) are in free float.

# ORLEN BRAND

The ORLEN brand has, for the last seven years, consistently topped the Rzeczpospolita ranking list of the most valuable Polish brands. Last year alone saw the value of the ORLEN brand gain more than PLN 100 m, reaching a total of PLN 3.9 bn. Since the ORLEN brand first appeared on the market in 2000, the Company has consistently strength- ened its potential and position by communicating a coherent corporate identity.

The ORLEN Group’s flagship is its service stations network, operating in Poland and Lithuania under the ORLEN brand in the premium segment, and under the BLISKA brand in the economy segment. PKN ORLEN is also well known for its engine oils, operating fluids, lubricants and other products for motor vehicles manufactured by the ORLEN Group companies.

Market research shows that our customers highly value the services and products offered under the ORLEN brand for their accessibility, guaran- teed high quality and standard of customer service. We seek to consistently broaden and improve our non-fuel offering in the ORLEN premium segment not only through the well-stocked stores at our service stations, but also catering and fast food outlets, as well as new additional services under the FLOTA programme.

The ORLEN brand is also promoted through our involvement in major conference events, which are important venues for public discussion on key economic and social issues in a domestic and international context. As part of the Future Fuelled by Knowledge project, PKN ORLEN representatives participated in the key debates held last year at the European Financial Congress, the European Forum of New Ideas, and the Economic Forum in Krynica. Our repre- sentatives were present to observe the exchange of ideas and recommendations at the World Economic Forum in Davos, and the St. Petersburg International Economics Forum. They also attended CERAWeek in Houston, where energy sector leaders discussed many strategic issues and challenges facing the industry. The ORLEN brand is also associated with the sponsorship of sport, culture and art events, as well as social and edu- cational projects.

## PKN ORLEN Annual Report 2013

## 16


---
*Page 18*


Consistent building of new business areas with financial stability maintained

When we commenced the work on a new strategy for 2013–2017, we knew that in the course of its implementation we would have to face a volatile, changing environment. It was already evident back then that the European refinery market was under a great deal of pressure from the macroeconomic background. At the same time, estimates concern- ing the grey market for fuels in Poland were rising at an alarming rate, especially in diesel oil.

Thus, last year we had to tackle the exceptionally adverse impact of this operating environment on our core business. The illegal trade in fuels, with market share currently estimated at around 15%, remains a major issue for the industry. While our southern neighbours have success- fully implemented regulations to address this problem, Polish legislation in this area still needs further development. Companies operating in compliance with applicable tax regulations will find it difficult to maintain their competitive edge and halt declines in performance unless new legal solutions to curb the grey fuels market in Poland are implemented.

Another issue is regulations governing the main- tenance of mandatory stocks. The existing legal regime, under which companies are obliged to maintain stocks covering 76-day consumption, puts a burden on their balance sheets and has an indirect effect on fuel prices paid by customers. As at the end of 2013, the ORLEN Group main- tained mandatory stocks valued at PLN 7.2 bn. The work on amending the Act on Mandatory Stocks is nearing completion and we hope that the solutions adopted will take into account the position of the industry.

At a European level, there was much work on new legislation concerning the climate policy. From our perspective, it is crucial that climate- related regulations are closely linked with business objectives. In the coming years, the role of natural

gas as an environmentally-friendly feedstock will undoubtedly grow. The ORLEN Group is among the largest consumers of gas fuel in Poland. Given our planned involvement in gas-fired energy gene­ ration projects, gas supplies will become increasingly more important to us. Therefore, it is essential that we actively participate in the process of change in the gas market. We are building relationships with gas suppliers both in Poland and abroad. While we acknowledge the significant potential benefits of deregulation in the Polish gas sector, we also expect that, without further expansion of the infrastructure (including in particular cross- border connections) the growth of the market will be slow and insufficient. It also important to com- panies purchasing gas that the problem of long- term contracts be solved through regulations which would enable them to meet a part of their gas requirement with purchases on the energy exchange, outside binding contracts.

Last year, we continued our efforts to build our new business segment: exploration for and production of hydrocarbons. The upstream business has become a pillar of our strategy, and in the coming years hydrocarbon production, from both unconventional and conventional deposits, will be further developed. To date, we have drilled ten exploratory wells and performed two hydraulic fracturing treatments. The plans provide for further extensive work under our licences in Poland. Last year, we extended the area of our shale gas exploration activities. Having finalised the transac- tion with Exxon Mobil at the beginning of 2013, we now own ten licences in the country. In line with our strategy, we also monitored the market for M&A opportunities, and at the end of 2013 we finalised the acquisition of Canadian Tri Oil Resources. This transaction is vital for the imple- mentation of the strategy, and with it we have gained access to production assets and unique expertise, which we intend to use in shale gas exploration in Poland.

### PROGRESS OF THE IMPLEMENTATION OF THE ORLEN GROUP STRATEGY

## 17

*Page 19*


The power generation segment is an important element in PKN ORLEN’s value building process. In mid-2013, we commenced the construction of a 463 MWe CCGT unit. An important feature of the project is its location, close to industrial customers for heat and electricity such as ANWIL, which has a positive effect on the economic viability of the project. The construction of a CCGT unit in Płock is another project proposed in this area, and the final investment decision is expected in the second half of 2014. Our involvement in power generation is based on the forecast of growing electricity demand in Poland com- bined with diminishing domestic production capacities. We anticipate PKN ORLEN’s installed generation capacity to grow from the current level of 345 MWe to 1,400 MWe by 2017. Planned investments in the power segment are expected to provide a stable revenue stream as early as 2017.

Last-year’s achievements also included a maintained strong position in the petrochemical segment in our region, and leadership in the production of olefins and polyolefins. The forecast increase in demand for petrochemical products gives a solid foundation for further value growth in this area, with priority to be given to maximising value from the existing assets. In line with the strategy, the petrochemical segment intends to leverage PKN ORLEN’s potential by stepping up production at key units, enhancing olefin production efficiency and boosting polymer and PTA sales.

We also consistently pursue our objectives in the retail segment, which recorded its best ever performance in 2013 by leveraging a strong and well-established market position. Not only did we increase fuel sales, but we also invested to deve­lop our non-fuel offering. In Q 4 2013 alone, 83 new Stop Cafe and Stop Cafe Bistro catering outlets were opened at ORLEN service stations. For the coming years we plan to spend more in further expansion of the network, primarily through the construction of motorway stations.

Last year proved extremely difficult in the refi­ning segment all over Europe, with refining margins sinking to ten-year lows in Q 4 2013. The segment’s performance was also adversely affected by the grey market, both in Poland and the Czech Republic. Although the ORLEN Group operates high-quality integrated production facilities, the adverse macro­ economic environment forced us to focus on further efficiency improvements and on maximising value based on our existing capabilities. The impact of the weaker refining sector’s performance was felt most at the Mažeikiai refinery, which was reflected in the results from ORLEN Lietuva.

On the Czech market, we acquired a 16.335% interest in Česká Rafinérská from Shell in 2013. After this transaction, Unipetrol, an ORLEN Group company, gained a qualified majority of votes and further control over the Czech refineries. As part of our activities in the Czech Republic, we secured supplies of crude oil to the Litvinov refinery by signing a long-term contract directly with an oil producer in June 2013. We see our presence on the Czech market as a long-term investment, and therefore it was paramount that we secure feedstock supplies to ensure the uninterrupted operation of the refinery.

# MACROECONOMIC SITUATION

## Five years after the Great Recession

Seeing the global economy struggle with the aftermath of the financial crisis, we are con- stantly on the look out for signs of recovery which would herald a return to growth. The amount of uncertainty we are dealing with today makes it difficult to formulate any clear-cut assessment.

Our consistent efforts and attention to the financial fundamentals have had results: we have regained investment-grade rating from leading agencies, Fitch and Moody’s, which assigned us credit ratings of BBB- and Baa 3, respectively, in 2013, with a ‘stable’ outlook. In line with our strategy of diversifying financing sources, we also carried out a very successful issue of retail bonds: we placed PLN 700 m worth of debt securities on the market, which met with enormous interest from retail investors.

Our liquidity position, financial performance and investments in areas of key importance to our operations are the foundations upon which we intend to further build value in our Company. Quite obviously, much depends on the external environment, including macroeconomic factors. For our industry, the poor economic situation is no longer a transitory state but a real economic environment, and we need to flexibly respond to what is happening. For this reason, in the coming years we intend to put even more emphasis on improving efficiency, while simultaneously investing in more promising areas in order to protect PKN ORLEN against fluctuations on the market or a prolonged economic slowdown in the refining sector.

# SŁAWOMIR JĘDRZEJCZYK

## Vice-President and CFO

## PKN ORLEN Annual Report 2013

## 18


---
*Page 20*


When reviewing the ORLEN Group’s results for the previous year, the first year of implementa- tion of the 2013–2017 strategy, it may be useful to have a look at the external environment in which the Group operates. We do not have control over factors such as crude oil prices, fuel and petrochemical product prices or foreign exchange rates. Changes in these factors, difficult to predict in a one-year span, may often impact our performance more than the consequences of our own actions.

The ORLEN Group operates in a global industry, so we should start with a review of the world economy, which accelerated, as expected, follow- ing the weak first quarter of 2013. The acceleration was driven by leading central banks announcing to keep interest rates low, by the easing of auster- ity policies and measures taken by EU institutions to reduce the eurozone risk, as well as − across the Atlantic − by the weaker than expected effects of tax cuts in the US.

If we look at European refineries, the newest of which is more than 30 years old, we will see that the strong pressure on margins, which PKN ORLEN also faced in 2013, is structural, so it is relatively long-term in nature. On the one hand, this pressure is created by large, highly advanced refineries coming on stream in the region with the fastest growing demand for fuels − namely those in Asia. Those refineries have lower pro- duction costs relative to their European peers, partly because of their lower labour costs and regulatory costs related to environmental and climate protection requirements. On the other hand, there are US. refineries, which are struc- turally similar to the European ones (aged but upgraded) and which, until quite recently, struggled with the same problems as those in Europe, but have been given a second chance by the shale revolution. Thanks to declining energy costs and lower crude oil prices, attributable to a fast growing supply and crude oil export ban still in place, fuel production costs fell dramatically in the US. The growing domestic production of fuels in America squeezed out imports from Europe, and most evidently so on the gasoline market, with gasoline surpluses forcing European refineries to cut back on production and increase unit pro- duction costs. If we also factor in the higher energy costs driven by the European climate policy and the resultant costs of regulation directly affecting refineries, it all adds up to a very unfavourable situation for European refiner for which there is no good solution within the sector itself: lower demand, more expensive crude oil, lower mar- gins, higher operating and development expenses.

At PKN ORLEN, we see these structural changes as an opportunity for our long-term growth. The awareness of this structural weakness of the European oil industry in its current form has been reflected in the ORLEN Group’s development strategy, which provides for expansion into new segments and new markets. It is thanks to this flexible approach that the Group is being transformed from regional oil company into a global energy player.

## Andrzej Kozłowski

## Executive Director,

## Strategy and Project Management

These changes, in which we have just started to invest, and their effects are not yet reflected in our annual figures, but they will make our results less vulnerable to external factors outside our control.

An important factor that caused an outflow of capital from commodity markets and emerging economies in 2013 and the resulting fall in commodity prices and weakening of emerging-market currencies, was the Fed’s announcement in June that it may begin to scale back its quantitative easing programme by the end of 2013. This announcement alone was enough to trigger a temporary price decline on the oil market, which was quickly reversed on the back of geopolitical tensions in Africa and the Middle East. However, the recovery was not spread evenly across all markets.

## Adam Czyżewski

## Chief Economist

Developed economies saw a marked improvement and had a larger share of GDP growth, as opposed to emerging economies which reported a visible slowdown in growth due to limited international capital inflow. Among emerging economies, there were those that operated in a sustainable manner, including Poland, and those which were in a less favourable macroeconomic situation and more sensitive to economic cycles.

The improvement in the global and European economies failed to benefit the European refining sector, for which 2013 was a year full of challenge. Refining margins fell far short of expectations and were much weaker than in the record-breaking 2012, and this negatively impacted our perfor- mance. Moreover, the temporarily positive Urals- Brent differential in the third quarter of the year was quite unexpected.

## CORPORATE SOCIAL 
RESPONSIBILITY

Each year PKN ORLEN publishes a CSR report prepared in line with the Global Reporting Initia- tive (GRI) standards, presenting a comprehensive economic, social and environmental overview of the ORLEN Group. In addition, the Company has made a voluntary commitment to respect in all spheres of its activity certain fundamental values, such as human rights, environmental protection and labour standards, and to counteract corruption.

As corporate social responsibility is one of the keys to efficient and effective achievement of goals, the ORLEN Group is focused on improvement in such areas as health care, safety and education. The range of initiatives is wide and includes support for existing and former employees and foster family group homes, as well as financing of scholarships, educational programmes and non-governmental organisations.

Ongoing care for the community is manifest in social campaigns run by the ORLEN – GIFT FROM THE HEART Foundation. These programmes are addressed to a vast group of beneficiaries, particu- larly with projects benefitting local communities. The Foundation, one of the first of its kind in Poland, pursues a mission that involves high-value added activities to ensure equal opportunities for children who are deprived of the care of their natural parents, as well as active partnership with local communities. This is achieved through the encouragement and support of young people in their education or pro- jects aimed at improving safety and health care.

For the ORLEN Group, corporate environmental responsibility is much more than just compliance with environmental laws and regulations or under­taking educational projects. It also encompasses imple- mentation of environmental management systems and investing in innovative, environment-friendly technologies which bring benefits and are a source of competitive advantage. Protection of the environ- ment is visible at all stages of the ORLEN Group’s operations. Our participation in the Responsible Care programme has helped us deliver on dozens of environmental commitments voluntarily made since we joined the programme in 1997. Also environmental education − of our employees as well as of our customers, suppliers and local communities − is a very important part of our pro- environmental efforts. For a more comprehensive description of environmental matters, see section 5 of this Report.

PKN ORLEN pursues a fully transparent policy of respect for human rights and non-discrimination. Our recruitment processes, in which all candidates are given equal opportunities, are run in conform- ity with applicable procedures and with corporate governance and CSR principles. Irrespective of age,

sex, workplace or experience, employees may par- ticipate in training and development programmes and are guaranteed equal access to a broad range of medical services extending beyond occupational medicine.

Respecting the dignity of the disabled, PKN ORLEN, as a Trustworthy Employer, places special emphasis on ensuring equal opportunities for the disabled at the workplace. PKN ORLEN creates appropriate conditions for the disabled to perform their work duties as well as opportunities for promotion, and removes social barriers at the workplace. For matters relating to our recruitment policy and HR programmes, see section 6 of this Report.

As a transparent organisation, PKN ORLEN actively promotes the fight against corruption. The document entitled ‘PKN ORLEN Code of Conduct’ compre- hensively covers all ethical issues and issues related to its anti-corruption procedures. An important role in the anti-corruption policy is played by the Ethics Committee and Ethics Officer as well as by the well-developed system for reporting abuse.

In 2013, in recognition of its high CSR standards, PKN ORLEN was awarded the ‘Silver CSR Leaf’ for the second time running. The Company also took first place in the ‘Fuel, Power Generation and Hydrocarbon Production’ category in the seventh edition of the prestigious ranking list of Socially Responsible Companies, evaluating the largest Polish companies in terms of the quality of their CSR management policies.

## PKN ORLEN’s CSR initiatives

PKN ORLEN adheres to the principles of corporate social responsibility relying, amongst other things, on the ‘PKN ORLEN Code of Conduct’. This docu- ment, adopted by the Company’s Management Board in September 2012, defined a new objective for the Company and its employees. The Code’s values have been defined in response to current challenges, and support the implementation of the ORLEN Group’s strategy. Responsibility, Growth, People, Energy and Reliability are the values that determine the path to achievement of ambi- tious goals by the organisation and by its people.

The organisation is undergoing change, yet some aspects remain constant. One of these is PKN ORLEN’s participation, for more than ten years, in GLOBAL COMPACT, an initiative proposed by the UN Secretary General. The initiative’s ten principles in the areas of human rights, labour standards, environmental protection and anti- corruption measures have for many years been priorities valued by the Company.

# SOCIAL SPONSORSHIP

The ORLEN Group’s social sponsorship activity focused on several selected areas.

The first of these was the sponsorship of pro- jects promoting national culture. The Company committed its resources to a number of initia- tives related to film, theatre, music and literature. The most important of the projects in this category included the 38 th Gdynia Film Festival, digitisation of the most important Polish films, Grand Theatre – Polish National Opera, the ‘Da! Da! Da!’ Festival, and ‘Theatre Confrontations’ international festival. The ORLEN Group provided support to the Ludwig van Beethoven Easter Festival and Krzysztof Penderecki Festival, as well as to celebra- tions of the Witold Lutosławski Year at the Warsaw Philharmonic. The Group also became the founder of the First International Zbigniew Herbert Award in the ‘Otwartym tekstem’ literature contest organised by the Chimera literary monthly magazine.

Another important area of CSR activities was in education promotion. The Scholarship Fund continued to focus on the most talented stu- dents from the town and region of Płock. Another initiative we pursued was the ‘Masters of Chemistry’ scholarship programme, dedicated to secondary school students having a talent for sciences. Another 1,000 lessons were given in 500 secondary schools throughout the country as part of our original educational programme ‘ORLEN’s Chemistry Class’.

Another strategic CSR initiative is our ‘ORLEN − Safe Roads’ project, which has been running since 2006. The purpose of the project is to improve safety on Polish roads. The activities undertaken in 2013 focused on issues related to the shared use of roads by motorcyclists and drivers. We promoted good driving standards and mutual respect among all road users. The cam- paign’s motto was ‘Long live motorcyclists! Look in the rear view mirror!’. It was accompanied by a social campaign run in the media and at PKN ORLEN service stations during the summer months, when the traffic on Polish roads intensifies.

PKN ORLEN’s sponsorship efforts were most visible in the geographical areas of its business activity. Traditionally it was Płock, where the ORLEN Group’s head office and largest manufacturing plant are site, but also Ostrów Wielkopolski, which is the site of one of the most modern fuel distribution depots in Europe. An area that has become important to us and is a beneficiary of our CSR projects is the Lublin region, mainly because of the shale gas exploration projects cur- rently underway there. In those regions we have implemented the largest number of CSR projects.

As part of ‘ORLEN for Płock’, a dedicated CSR pro- gramme, the ORLEN Group continued its spon- sorship of the ORLEN Wisła Płock handball team and held the naming rights for the ORLEN Arena Sports and Performance Hall in Płock. The ORLEN Wisła Płock team is a ten-time winner of the Polish Cup and a seven-time winner of the Polish Championship. It has also been runner-up twelve times and third place five times. Every year, it is a serious contender for the trophies. It has also competed in the Champions League and the IHF and EHF Cup. Last season, the team included play- ers who were also members of the Polish national team or who played with the Hungarian, Serbian, Croatian, Norwegian, Bosnian-Herzegovinian and Romanian national teams. Since 2013, the team has been coached by Manolo Cadenas, the coach of the Spanish national team, which won the Men’s World Handball Championship in the same year. The Płock handball players always have thousands of fans at the ORLEN ARENA as they compete in the Champions League or national tournaments.

We also focused a great deal on the disabled and provided support to Polish Paralympians and the Polish Special Olympic Games Society, as well as to the international ORLEN Polish Open wheel- chair tennis tournament, held in Płock.

To promote awareness of social issues, PKN ORLEN has launched ‘Extraordinary People’, a Facebook page addressed to those wanting to help. In 2013, the page was liked by more than 50 thousand Facebook users. Such an initiative contributes to the growth of social activity, and the ORLEN Group now has an interesting and engaging platform for dialogue with its stakeholders.

In 2014, we will continue to focus on projects embodying values of the ORLEN Group, namely Responsibility, Development, People, Energy and Reliability. We will promote sports, culture, arts, knowledge and education, and provide continued support to most important social projects.

As part of its brand building efforts, the Company also focused on several carefully selected themes including support for sports projects.

# SPORTS SPONSORSHIP

Sport plays a crucial role in our sponsorship strategy. On the one hand, as the largest Polish company we take responsibility for supporting disciplines benefitting millions of Polish people, and on the other hand, we are aware that sport has enormous marketing potential. A combina- tion of those two approaches benefits selected disciplines and individual athletes as well as our primary brand and individual trading brands. Our sports sponsorship and association of our brands with individual disciplines and athletes ensure that the brands come to be identified with the best qual- ities of Polish sport and enthusiasm in its support. PKN ORLEN has for years focused on sports that best convey our corporate spirit and the nature of our industry. This is why we sponsor such disciplines as athletics, volleyball and motorsports.

## ORLEN Sports Group

Our cooperation with Poland’s leading athletes as part of the ORLEN Sports Group is a platform for promotional activities enabling us to conduct product marketing campaigns to strengthen the Stop Cafe brand. The Sports Group comprises Tomasz Majewski (shot-put), Piotr Małachowski (discus), Anita Włodarczyk (hammer), Adam Kszczot and Marcin Lewandowski (800 metres), and Henryk Szost (marathon). Nearly 70% of Polish people declare an interest in volleyball, which continues to be one of the most keenly watched sports. By sponsoring volleyball, the ORLEN brand gains exposure to a vast audi- ence and the passions associated with this sport come to be associated with our brand.

This sport also enables effective promotion of fuel and non-fuel products at service stations (e.g. volleyball promotion), acquisition of new customers (promotion of the Stop Cafe and the VITAY scheme at volleyball tournaments) and the sup- port of B 2 B sales (VIP Hospitality).

# ORLEN’S OWN PROJECTS

## ORLEN Warsaw Marathon

ORLEN Warsaw Marathon is an event that pro- motes an active lifestyle with people of all ages and abilities and appeals not only to keen runners but also entire families. The first edition of the event, on April 20 th –21 st 2013, attracted over 26,000 par- ticipants, supported by many thousands of spectators in the runners’ centre and along the marathon route itself. ORLEN Warsaw Marathon – National Running Day became an exceptional sports week- end. On the first day of the event, competitors took part in a 3 km charity run. For each runner enrolled to run the distance, the ORLEN – GIFT FROM THE HEART Foundation donated PLN 10 for sports education of children in foster family group homes. The main part of the event, includ- ing the 10 km run and the marathon itself took place on Sunday. The event was also the 83 rd edition of the Polish Men’s Marathon. TV coverage of the ORLEN Warsaw Marathon was watched in total by 23 m people, while 97% of the specta- tors and 92% of the runners declared their willing- ness to participate in the next edition of the event.

## PKN ORLEN Annual Report 2013

## 24


---
*Page 26*


## Poles with Verve

PKN ORLEN initiated the ‘Poles with Verve’ award to show our country’s potential and reward the most gifted and creative people in Poland whose passion and commitment contribute to building a modern country. In a public vote carried out at PKN ORLEN service stations, on the voting website and on the VITAY loyalty scheme website, more than 330,000 votes were cast for young academics, creators and businesspeople nominated in the fol- lowing categories: Arts and Sciences, Medicine, Arts and Culture, Environmental Protection, Business Innovation, Design and Sport. The voting ended in a ceremonial gala at the Grand Theatre – Polish National Opera in Warsaw where the winners were presented with statuettes made by Paweł Althamer, as well as grants to help them finance their future professional development. Category winners were as follows: Business Innovation – Rafał Brzoska; Environmental Protection – Anna Dębicka; Sport – Jerzy Janowicz; Arts and Culture – Katarzyna Krakowiak; Medicine – Wojciech Fendler, Ph D; Arts and Sciences – Prof. Janusz Bujnicki, Ph D; and Design – Jan Strumiłło. By a decision of the jury, a Special Award was given to surgeon Prof. Adam Maciejewski and his team, who performed a face transplant. This was the first face transplant in Poland and first-in- the-world life-saving face transplantation. The gala event at the Grand Theatre – Polish National Opera in Warsaw had an audience of 1,500, including many from the world of business, arts and culture, and politics, and the event also attracted television viewers of over 0.5 m.

## VERVA Racing Team

The VERVA Racing Team is the first profession- al team in Poland driving a Porsche 911 GT 3 Cup car in the fastest mono-brand racing series in the world as part of the Formula 1 Grand Prix weekends.

The year 2013 was the fourth season of the VERVA Racing Team’s participation in the Porsche Super- cup series. Team successes included third place in the 2011 final team classification, fourth place in 2013, and many podium finishes and high positions in final individual classification. In 2013, the VERVA Racing Team was represented by Kuba Giermaziak, a member of the team since its incep- tion, and Patryk Szczerbiński, enjoying his second season in team colours.

Competing on the Formula 1 circuit in the Por- sche Supercup series wins prestige for the team and is a great opportunity to promote Polish mot- orsports. The event is an exciting, high-speed and high-adrenalin spectacle to watch. The proximity of the king of motorsports attracts much media interest, thousands of spectators in the stands and millions of viewers in front of their TV screens.

## VERVA Street Racing

In 2013, the fourth edition of the largest motor- sports event in Central and Eastern Europe was for the first time held at the National Stadium in Warsaw. This special edition of VERVA Street Racing combined three formats: the Pit Party – a show of nearly 300 unique vehicles, exhibition races in several motorsport disciplines, and Top Gear Live hosted by the three presenters from the popular British motor programme.

The ORLEN Team and VERVA Racing Team com- petitors, as well as the ORLEN Sports Group athletes took part in the show at the National Stadium.

VERVA Street Racing 2013 was viewed by 58,000 fans at the National Stadium, and nearly 4 m televi- sion viewers. ORLEN Team

ORLEN Team is undoubtedly one of the key projects that benefit PKN ORLEN, have a posi- tive effect on perception of the brand and enable us to maximise the opportunities offered by our involvement in sponsoring motorsports.

In January 2013, the ORLEN Team once again took part in the Dakar Rally. The first Polish team that started the struggle in the desert included three motorcyclists: Kuba Przygoński, Jacek Czachor and Marek Dąbrowski, as well as Szymon Ruta as a driver. At the end, the ORLEN Team was thoroughly reorganised – Dąbrowski and Czachor moved from their single track vehicles to a car to set up a car crew.

The team was also joined by Adam Małysz and Rafał Marton. In the 2013 season, Jakub Przygoński took part in five rallies, including the Abu Dhabi Desert Challenge, and rallies in Qatar, Sardinia, Brazil, and Morocco, and finally achieved third place in general classification of the FIM Cross- Country Rallies World Championship.

Both car crews, namely Dąbrowski/Czachor and Małysz/Marton, participated together in eight rallies, including the Silk Road Rally considered the second most challenging rally in the world, with only Dakar above it. At the end of the season, the Małysz/Marton crew won the Central Europe- an Zone Championship and the title of Runner-Up of Poland. The Dąbrowski/Czachor crew were among the top four in all rallies they participated in (their first season) and had an outstanding second place in the Baja Poland Rally. 2013 saw the start of the ORLEN Team Academy whose purpose is to find talented new motorbike riders to join the ORLEN Team.

### PROGRESS OF THE IMPLEMENTATION OF THE ORLEN GROUP STRATEGY

## 25


---
*Page 27*


In 2013, a major change in the ownership struc- ture of the ORLEN Group was the establishment of ORLEN Upstream International, a subsidiary, in September 2013. The company acquired 100% of shares in Tri Oil Resources in November 2013. Further, under an agreement with Shell Overseas executed in November 2013, Unipetrol purchased additional shares in Česká Rafinérská, as a result of which its interest in Česká Rafinérská’s share capital rose to 67.56%.

Other changes consisted in increasing PKN ORLEN’s equity interests in ORLEN Oil, ORLEN Asfalt and ORLEN Transport to 100% in order to strengthen control over the companies. PKN ORLEN also consistently increased its holdings in ORLEN Projekt, ORLEN Laboratorium and ORLEN Centrum Serwisowe. ANWIL followed a similar investment policy, increasing its equity interests in Rem Will, Chemeko and Prof-Med, while ORLEN Lietuva sold its shares in UAB Medikvita.

 GROUP
 STRUCTURE

The ORLEN Group comprises PKN ORLEN (the Parent) and companies based in Poland, Germany, the Czech Republic, Lithuania, Malta, Sweden, the Netherlands, Slovakia, Switzerland, Estonia, Latvia and Canada.

As at December 31 st 2013, PKN ORLEN held, directly or indirectly, shares in 90 companies, including:

• 78 subsidiaries, • 7 jointly-controlled entities, and • 5 associated entities.

# RETAIL

The excellent performance of our retail segment reflects the growing efficiency of our service station network, accurate identification of market trends and prompt implementation of adequate solutions. We worked to improve our non-fuel sales by extending the network of catering outlets and by launching new services.

Aware of the enormous growth potential of the motorway station segment, we paid much attention to this format and have remained the market leader in terms of the number of motorway service areas built in Poland.

## Marek Podstawa

## Member of the Management Board, Sales

In 2013, despite the tough market conditions, our retail segment reported its highest ever EBITDA of PLN 1.3 bn. The ORLEN Group also strength- ened its position in Poland, the Czech Republic and Lithuania, increasing its respective market shares. Our non-fuel sales continued to grow dynamically: in 2013, we openned 234 new Stop Cafe and Stop Cafe Bistro outlets, bringing the total to 1,047.

With a network of 2,706 service stations, we are a leader on the fuel market in the region. In Poland, our service stations operate under the ORLEN brand in the premium segment and the BLISKA brand in the economy segment. In the Czech Republic, we use the BENZINA PLUS and BENZINA brands, respectively, and in Lithuania − the ORLEN Lietuva and VENTUS brands. On the German market, our service stations operate mainly in the economy segment, using the STAR brand.

## PKN ORLEN Annual Report 2013

## 28

*Page 30*


In order to transform traditional service stations into multi-function amenities, new solutions and services need to be implemented, and the range of products and services offered by the stores and catering outlets needs to be developed.

In 2013, the number of service stations on the ORLEN Group’s markets did not change materially, with new sites replacing stations closed as unprofitable or not meeting technological requirements. However, the number of inde- pendent stations fell as independent operators are more frequently opting to cooperate with major international service station networks. In Central and Eastern Europe, a steady increase has also been observed in fuel sales from hypermarket service stations (ca. 530 stations on the ORLEN Group’s markets), which puts additional pressure on the traditional operators. In H 2 2013, legal regulations were enacted in Poland and the Czech Republic, designed to eliminate the grey market, or illegal trade in fuels in those countries.

In 2013, the consumption of fuels in Poland, the Czech Republic and Lithuania decreased year on year. The only increase reported in the period was growth in the volume of diesel oil sales in the developed and stable German market.

In 2013, the ORLEN Group recorded a 0.6% year-on-year increase in sales volumes, to 9.5 bn litres. The strongest growth was seen in middle distillates, which is mainly attributable to strong sales on the German market.

## Poland

As at the end of 2013, the ORLEN Group oper- ated a network of 1,778 service stations in Poland, including 1,263 stations under the ORLEN brand in the premium segment and 358 stations under the BLISKA brand in the economy segment. Our investment programme, which focuses on opening new and modernising the existing locations, developing our catering services, and loyalty schemes, helped us strengthen our leading posi- tion and increase our share of the Polish retail fuel market by 0.7 pp, to 35.4%.

PKN ORLEN’s main competitors in Poland include Shell, BP, Lotos, Statoil and Lukoil.

Our retail network was extended by the addition of 11 new sites, bringing the total number of our service stations to 1,778 at the end of 2013. In 2013, the ORLEN Group continued to de- velop its franchise network, increasing the number of DOFO stations to 440 (11 more than in 2012). The share of DOFO stations in the network stood at around 25%. In 2013, 16 new CODO service stations were opened, including 4 motorway service areas. Thirty-six CODO stations were completely rebuilt as part of a project to upgrade the existing sites. At the same time, following detailed business analyses, 16 service stations were closed down.

---

2013 was the last year to bring service stations in compliance with the new environmental-protection standards effective since the beginning of 2014. It is estimated that the new regulations will result in the closing down of around 10% of the service stations in operation in 2013. This would mainly affect ‘no name’ stations, because the majority of large net- works have already adapted their facilities to the new requirements in recent years.

Taking into consideration customers’ growing expectations and changes on the fuel market, we resolved to re-brand the BLISKA stations and incorporate them into the ORLEN brand. In 2013, the rebran­ding covered 125 sites. Other developments included continued modernisation of the service stations, ensuring availability of free Internet access at all Stop Cafes, and the ‘Station with a Parcel’ service (parcel collection points) available at 650 sites on a 24/7 basis, addressed primarily to customers who appreciate flexibility and convenience.

A strategic objective for 2014 is to build new service areas on motorways and expressways. We currently operate 29 service areas located on the A 1, A 2 and A 4 motorways and on express- ways, of which 19 are already fully operational.

Despite adverse market trends, in 2013 fuel sales volume was only slightly lower than in 2012, and amounted to 5.7 bn litres.

Increased efficiency was also reported: the aver- age annual sales volume reached 3.5 m litres per station, up 1% on 2012. Offering the possibility of paying the motorway toll charge with fleet cards, making the VITAY loyalty programme available to fleet customers and the success of the Biznes Tank programme, proved vital in reaching new customer groups and increasing the fleet sales volume by over 4 pp year on year, to almost 30%. Biznes Tank is a fleet programme dedicated to business customers, small and medium-sized enterprises, which buy at least 500 litres of fuels monthly. Towards the end of 2013, the Mikro Flota project was also launched. It is expected to reach Polish microenterprises to which fuel cards are currently not available.

Revenue from sales of non-fuel products and services did not change significantly year on year. Despite the growing competition from discount stores, the average non-fuel sales per PKN ORLEN’s own service station grew by over 3% year on year.

2013 was another year of further development of catering services in our network. Through a continuous effort to broaden the offering and adapt it to customers’ changing expectations, we achieved an over 5% year-on-year increase in revenue from catering services. The number of Stop Cafe and Stop Cafe Bistro outlets rose by 234 in 2013, to 1,047 at year end.

The high standard of service at PKN ORLEN’s service stations and customers’ appreciation of the network were confirmed by the awards and distinctions we received in 2013. Evidence of the success of our catering concept was the first place taken by our service station in Gnilec in the ‘Catering at a Service Station’ category of the ‘Service Station of 2013’ contest organ- ised by Brog Marketing. PKN ORLEN was also awarded the ‘Customer Service Quality Star 2013’ in the Service Stations category under the Polish Customer Service Quality Programme, and the ‘Trustworthy Brand’ title in the survey conducted by Reader’s Digest.

## Germany

Germany has one of the largest and most mature retail fuel markets in Europe. As at December 31 st 2013, ORLEN Deutschland Gmb H operated 555 service stations (535 in the economy seg- ment under the STAR brand, 19 service stations at supermarkets, and one ORLEN brand station in Hamburg).The company’s major competitors include international networks Aral, Shell, Esso, Total and JET. ORLEN Deutschland Gmb H’s STAR brand is the second largest, after JET, in the econ- omy segment in Germany. In 2013, the ORLEN Group maintained its position on the German retail market, with a 5.9% market share.

On the back of stable demand for fuels, ORLEN Deutschland Gmb H’s year-on-year sales improved 1.4%, to 3.2 bn litres, with the growth driven pri- marily by higher sales of middle distillates.

The continued development of the STAR network, including in particular the launch of the company’s own beverage brands, contributed to a 13% year-on-year increase in non-fuel revenue. An important factor supporting the network devel- opment and customer base growth was the launch of the Star VISA payment card in cooperation with a German bank.

## Czech Republic

As at December 31 st 2013, Benzina s.r.o. man- aged a network of 338 service stations in the Czech Republic, operating primarily in the economy segment (198 BENZINA stations) and the premium segment (113 BENZINA PLUS stations), as well as 27 service stations operating under the simplified model.

Benzina’s successful operating activities contributed to a 1.1 pp growth in its market share, to 14.7%. OMV, Shell, Euro Oil and Lukoil, as well as ser- vice stations operated by hypermarkets continue to be the company’s main competitors.

2013 saw a continued decline in fuel consumption on the Czech market. However, despite the adverse market trends, Benzina’s retail sales grew by 5.0% year on year in 2013, to 0.6 bn litres. Thanks to marketing campaigns, more effective price management and initiatives enhancing the network’s image, Benzina reported year-on-year increase in revenue from non-fuel sales of 5%. As at the end of 2013, the company’s non-fuel offering included 92 service stations with catering areas operating in the Stop Cafe or Stop Cafe Bistro formats.

## Lithuania

The retail network in Lithuania is operated by AB Ventus Nafta and at December 31 st 2013 it com- prised 35 service stations, including 26 CODO and 9 DOFO stations. The stations operated under the ORLEN brand in the premium segment (23 stations) and the Ventus brand in the economy segment (12 stations). AB Ventus Nafta’s main competitors in this segment are Lukoil, Statoil, and Neste. As at the end of 2013, AB Ventus Nafta held a 3.4% share of the retail market.

2013 saw a 1.6% year-on-year increase in the company’s sales volumes, to almost 0.1 bn litres. A noteworthy development was the impro­ ving popularity of the VERVA diesel oil, with its sales rising by almost 7% year on year.

The development of catering services and effec- tive management of the non-fuel product portfolio enabled AB Ventus Nafta to markedly increase its revenue from non-fuel sales, by over 12% year on year.

# SALES OF REFINING 
PRODUCTS

In 2013, refineries across Europe were under a strong pressure from adverse macroeconomic conditions, including the lowest refining margins and Brent-Urals differential in more than ten years. Throughout the year, we pursued a number of optimisation initiatives to limit the impact of these factors.

We are fully aware of the challenges the refining sector will have to face, and therefore we will strive to further improve our refineries’ operating efficiency.

VOLUME OF REFINING PRODUCTS SOLD BY THE ORLEN GROUP [‘000 tonnes]

2013 saw a decline in the basic macroeconomic indicators and deterioration of the conditions on fuel markets relative to the previous year. Decelerating growth of GDP in the ORLEN Group’s markets led to lower consumption of fuels in these coun- tries. Additionally, diesel oil consumption was affected by the grey market, primarily in Poland and in the Czech Republic.

In 2013, the ORLEN Group conducted wholesale of refining products in Poland, the Czech Republic, Germany, Slovakia, Hungary, Lithuania, Latvia, Estonia, Finland and Ukraine, as well as (by sea) at West-European cargo-handling terminals.

In Poland, the wholesale of the ORLEN Group’s products is carried out by: PKN ORLEN, the ORLEN Asfalt Group, the Rafineria Nafty Jedlicze Group, the Rafineria Trzebinia Group, the ORLEN Oil Group, IKS SOLINO S.A., ORLEN Paliwa Sp. z o.o., ORLEN Petro Tank Sp. z o.o., Petrolot Sp. z o.o., ORLEN Gaz Sp. z o.o.,

and SHIP-SERVICE S.A. On foreign markets, the wholesale operations are conducted by: Unipetrol Slovensko s.r.o., Unipetrol Rafinerie s.r.o., Mogul Slovakia s.r.o., Paramo Oil s.r.o. and Paramo a.s. − (Unipetrol Group companies operating in the Czech Republic), ORLEN Lietuva Group companies: AB ORLEN Lietuva and UAB Mazeikiu naftos prekybos namai in Lithuania, SIA ORLEN Latvija in Latvia, and OU ORLEN Eesti in Estonia.

In 2013, the refining segment’s wholesale volumes increased 2.7% year on year, to 23,198 thousand tonnes, i.e. 64% of the ORLEN Group’s total sales volume. Higher sales of light and middle distil- lates were driven by rising sales volumes in Poland and ORLEN Lietuva’s markets. In the Czech Republic, the sales volumes declined. The higher sales of heavy fractions reflect increased volumes of heavy fuel oil sold in Poland and in the Czech Republic.

## Poland

Despite a challenging macroeconomic environment, including a of the consumption of fuels and adverse impact of the grey market, in 2013 the volume of refining products sold on the domestic wholesale market rose 3.3% year on year.

Improved sales of light distillates (up 9.4% year on year) followed from stronger sales of gaso- line on the back of new contracts signed by PKN ORLEN with the operators of large service station networks in Poland.

I, proved sales of middle distillates (up 3.6% year on year) were chiefly the effect of growing sales of diesel oil, accompanied by lower sales of light fuel oil and Jet A-1 aviation fuel. The large sales volumes of diesel oil reported despite the lower consumption and the impact of the grey market, were achieved through a flexible pricing policy, reflecting the market trends.

The challenging environment for the refining industry necessitated a series of initiatives to reinforce the position of the ORLEN Group.

Increasing the equity interest in Petrolot Sp. z o.o. has opened new prospects for sales of aviation fuels and airport services.
